The independent reference inventory now contains 185 admitted upstream rendered/captured frames: 44 gloo, 17 napari images/labels, 11 scalar volumes, six categorical volumes, nine scenes/fonts, three texture revisions, six topology cases, four Glue cases, twelve typed color captures, four depth captures, four stencil-semantic captures, fifteen line cases, twelve line extensions, two scoped DIVE image/export cases, one opaque-plugin retirement case, five plugin overlay cases, two unscheduled NumPy 2.4.6 plane cases, four public scheduled plane cases, fifteen original wide/smooth cases, one distinct depth-initialized wide case and eight point-identity/instancing cases. The following chronological entries retain earlier results and then-pending work; the current milestones above supersede their status without discarding their evidence.

`native-pass-8.log/.xml` report thirteen additional telemetry/categorical/stress cases passing with zero exit status. The first clean wheel (`bbf42127bf754f4950ce8e30bb384d91e69d7b5c015bbf056d5ac78658c809b1`) remains unqualified and preserved under `build/w1/artifact`. Its actual Text shaders exposed helper parameters misclassified as stage inputs, sampler parameters requiring Vulkan resource splitting, and a reserved helper name. `cpu-harness-pass-5.log` records eight passing CPU harnesses, including actual Tint reader, portable/device artifact and exhaustive half-float/color capture contracts. `capture-artifact-pass-1.log/.xml` records16 passing tests with exit zero: twelve independent typed captures, two analytic/metadata checks and two actual shader recovery tests. The tested native binary SHA256 is `9947b54c6ea124a28ffa105fba7881f68f986aba48c231ff5f83421519e5a57a`. `cpu-python-pass-5.log/.xml` records70 passing Python contract/metric tests; it also rechecks the separately frozen wheel, so it is not a CPU-only run or a qualification of the current runtime wheel.

The current source adds ordered region copies, fixed allocation storage across source dtype changes, shared GPU allocation identity for sampler clones, recursive loss invalidation and upload commit only after submission. All three independent texture revision cases pass (`build/reports/revision-pass-1.log/.xml`). The older `cpu-harness-pass-3.log` records six passing harnesses. Five fan cases and the Shapes fixture match upstream exactly. The then-failing loop differed at one corner pixel; `line-loop-endpoint-diagnosis.json` preserves that analysis. The later `line-bresenham-pass-1.log/.xml` records its exact match and exit zero using queried Vulkan Bresenham support with Dawn's fixed one-pixel dynamic width. No reference or tolerance changed. The fifteen new upstream line cases await their candidate run; a complete current-build qualification remains pending.

`line-depth-stencil-pass-2.log/.xml` records exact matches for all four upstream depth and four GL stencil-semantic captures; that run still had the older loop failure. Depth uses actual Vulkan D24 storage where available; stencil cropping copies the required whole subresource before CPU cropping. `cpu-harness-pass-7.log` records eight passing harnesses. `final-validation-metadata-plan.log` records 30 passing metadata/contract checks with 47 GPU cases deselected; those checks do not establish candidate rendering.

The two artifact review findings are now repaired. `compile_variant` uses the retained portable artifact's mapped original line and exposes separate vertex/fragment logs. One cache owns portable and device records, uses borrowed identity keys, accounts retained capacities, evicts whole records together and declines oversized admission. Live programs retain usable immutable artifacts outside that cache budget; separate live and uncached-byte counters expose that ownership. A regression prevents an evicted live device record from overwriting the accounting for its replacement. The [updated continuation plan](2026-10-09-vispy-vulkan-completion-plan.md) retains these checks for integration and fresh wheels.

`cpu-harness-pass-9.log` reports all eight current CPU harnesses passing. `artifact-lines-native-pass-2.log/.xml` reports 54 actual candidate tests passing: seven artifact cases, nine scene/font cases, fifteen initial lines, twelve extended lines, six original topology cases and metadata checks. All 27 additional line images match their independent GL frames exactly. Native pass 21 preserves legal named fragment outputs and uses actually queried `VK_EXT_provoking_vertex` LAST support for OpenGL flat interpolation; layer CPU contracts and native capability/counter assertions distinguish supported policy from an assumed default. No reference or tolerance changed.

`build/evidence/native-pass-21/artifacts.json` retains the matching extension (`c1fae0da17a970a918d82a3ebf5ff9e01bf4d2f94f244eadc902f8e87000a029`), layer (`eb0e57c7316f094fb042df2a861847612f703375881997053e02b38bc3dc400a`), manifest and source hashes. The dependency-verified build exits zero. `build/w4/source/wheel-source-manifest.json` freezes 574 project inputs with source-tree SHA256 `b767c6f752b723c6ffd7a7eb91f2e4080d4d4faf87001d7e55d948ff48de8b5d`; later source edits cannot qualify against this binary automatically.

The first integrated run (`integrated-native-pass-1.log/.xml`) records 198 passes and 36 failures. Napari's import calls public `vispy.use(gl='gl+')`, replacing the capability facade while its old installation sentinel remains set. Subsequent native-limit/sample queries incorrectly reach PyOpenGL without an OpenGL context. The Python repair restores Dawn queries after backend selection and checks the installed function itself. A meaningful existing/new-canvas regression exercises gl+, gl2, debug selection, granted limits, per-canvas sample counts, unsupported queries and real capture; `gl-facade-import-order-pass-1.log/.xml` reports 16 passing checks including the actual napari import sequence and all twelve extended lines.

The corrected `integrated-native-pass-2.log/.xml` reports 235 passes, zero failures, eight explicit unresolved upstream stencil-wrapper deselections and exit zero. Its recorded command selects every current native/gloo/shader/texture/state/marker/Qt/parity case except those eight admissions, all 23 admitted napari image/categorical-volume cases and the three actual presentation/completion cases. The diagnostic scan finds only the deliberate invalid-shader fixture's three compiler error lines; no unexpected validation, surface or teardown error appears. The binary/layer hashes match native pass 21, with the frozen Python repair in `build/w4/source`. This is integrated Windows/PyQt6 source evidence, not a current wheel, another host/binding/platform result or full release qualification.

Pinned DIVE/PySide2 captures expose removed private `_marker_funcs` usage in both scatter cases and an independent QApplication teardown access violation in image cases. A native stack and a CPU-only Qt reproduction locate the latter in DIVE's `QProxyStyle` ownership of Qt's shared application style. The explicitly scoped style-restoration harness yields two clean image/export references; their raw images and PNGs are byte-identical to prior failed-cleanup artifacts. These do not qualify ordinary unchanged DIVE teardown. Pinned napari-threedee has a genuine, byte-reproducible 0.0.29 wheel (`de115619e84a7967b0386651163c29ec12228d59e58000736067f066237ce91e`); its installed metadata discovers eleven widgets and resolves twelve commands. Public widget interaction, picking and rendering qualification is being prepared and remains open.

Native pass 9 failed device creation because the generated Windows Vulkan layer manifest used a loader-incompatible path. Reconfiguration fixes the installed manifest and restores actual Vulkan creation. Passes 10 and 11 subsequently expose an actual Text shader LINK access violation, while native trace confirms the marker scene passes. The narrowed Text crash is being debugged with retained native stacks rather than hidden by exclusions. Pass 12 ended during a Qt handle teardown callback: `effectiveWinId()` returned `None`. The current Python bridge treats that as a detached surface, and `hosts-pass-4.log/.xml` then pass all 23 image/label/categorical-volume cases with zero differences and exit zero. Full current-source qualification is still pending.

The symbolized first-chance stack and minidump under `build/evidence/text-native-debugger-map` place the Text failure in pinned Tint's SPIR-V texture lowering during Dawn shader-module creation. The debugger handles Windows venv redirector child processes and has independent CPU normal/crash/Unicode/argument smoke evidence. Passes 13 and 14 expose an additional late QWidget handle event after native canvas destruction; explicit closed-state guards now prevent that callback from addressing a removed canvas. The focused lifecycle rerun (`qt-lifecycle-pass-1.log/.xml`) passes both real parent/minimize/restore/recovery and handle-detach/late-close events. The parented presentation fixture now shows its parent to exercise an actual visible drawable. The diagnostic full runs excluded Text and loop coverage and remain incomplete, not passing qualification.

Complete the original scientific fixtures, host/plugin admissions, typed Vulkan/Metal surfaces, clean wheel/interpreter matrix and lifecycle checks. Extend allocation accounting to include retained derived buffers and transient/canvas resources. Add complete-frame telemetry and measure the fixed-quality resident/update workloads with the specified warmup, frame count and repetitions. Retain raw samples, provenance, counters and comparisons. Missing platform hardware or an upstream-incompatible host is an unresolved admission, never a passing skip.

The focused backend review finds a second concrete correction: HLSL writer.cc:102 and GLSL writer.cc:158 scan all IR instructions for helper queries before their Raise path prunes to the selected entrypoint. An unrelated helper entrypoint or never-called function can falsely reject ordinary selected code. Scope the guard to selected entry/callees and add mixed-entry, unused-function and declaration-order controls while retaining unsupported selected-helper refusal. SPIR-V/MSL guards match selected-entry metadata; their authored controls remain unrun. No held source is modified by this planning review.

The [new ordered implementation plan](2026-10-10-vispy-vulkan-next-batch-plan.md), linked from the updated [continuation plan](2026-10-10-vispy-vulkan-continuation-plan.md), puts both corrections first, followed by coherent generation/build/full compiler controls, the unchanged twelve-module complete ingestion probe, matching SDK/native integration and wide/smooth raster qualification.
